## Document 313: Mapping the World's Prices 2026 (2026-07-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Mapping the World's Prices 2026 (2026-07-13).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-07-13
Pages: 68
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Mapping the World's  
Prices - 2026

1 
Authors 
Jim Reid 
Global Head of Macro and  
Thematic Research

Galina Pozdnyakova 
Research Analyst

July 13, 2026

Which are the world’s cheapest and most expensive cities?  
Mapping the World’s Prices, now in its tenth edition, is the definitive guide to 
quality of life, rental costs and prices for phones, coffee, taxis and more in 69 
cities from Abu Dhabi to Zurich.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Mapping the World’s Prices – July 13, 2026

2 
Mapping the World’s Prices – 2026 
Welcome to the tenth edition of Mapping the World's Prices. When this series 
launched in 2012, the eurozone was in crisis, the yen was strong, and a 
cappuccino in Zurich already cost more than a full meal in Mumbai. Nearly 15 
years on, the global cost-of-living map has been redrawn – by a pandemic, an 
energy crisis, the strongest dollar cycle in a generation, and now by tariffs and 
geopolitical conflict. 
This year's edition covers 69 cities across 6 continents, with prices converted to 
US dollars and sourced primarily from Numbeo's crowdsourced database - 
augmented by our own cleaning and cross-checks. As always, crowdsourced 
data carries inherent noise: we flag potential outliers where they appear and urge 
readers to focus on broad trends rather than any single data point. 
In this edition, as well as looking at year-on-year changes, we compare 2026 
prices against 2016 levels - a full decade - as well as against 2019 (a pre-Covid 
baseline) and also against 2012 when we first published. 2012 was a pivotal year 
as it marked peak Yen and a historically cheap USD. So it’s a fascinating 
benchmark, especially as the exact reverse conditions are now in place. Reading 
this in 2012 would have probably led you to increase your exposure to the US. 
Will you want to do the same with Japan after reading 2026's edition? 
These longer-term perspectives reveal structural shifts that annual data alone 
cannot capture: which cities have genuinely become more expensive, where 
living standards have improved, and where an entire region's pricing has been 
fundamentally reset. 
If nothing else, it will give you a good idea of how expensive your summer holiday 
will be over the next few weeks and where you may want to go next year for the 
best value break! 
Please delve into the full report where we have synthesized tens of thousands of 
global price data points in user friendly summaries, graphs and tables. 
As always, feel free to quote and reference the report. If so we would be 
grateful if you could attribute to the Deutsche Bank Research Institute. Feel 
free to direct people to this report and many others at 
https://www.dbresearch.com/PROD/IE-PROD/HOME.alias

### Page 3

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Mapping the World’s Prices – July 13, 2026

At a Glance: The Headlines: 
— Zurich and Geneva continue to battle it out to be the most expensive cities 
in the world followed now by Tel Aviv, New York and San Francisco. Of the 
largest developed market cities, Tokyo stands out as being very “cheap” . 
— Luxembourg holds the #1 quality-of-life spot for a second year. 
Copenhagen, Amsterdam, Vienna and Munich are others in the top 5 with 
Germany also seeing Frankfurt in 6th. Global financial hubs like Paris (43rd), 
New York (46th), London (47th), and Hong Kong (55th), score lower on 
liveability, hampered by expensive housing, long commutes, and high 
pollution levels. However, this is a highly subjective measure. 
— Zurich tops the global salary table, followed by San Francisco, Geneva, 
Boston and New York. Over a decade, Budapest salaries have risen +161%, 
Prague +121%, and Warsaw +120%. 
— Cities that make the top 10 for both quality of life and disposable income 
are Luxembourg, Copenhagen, Frankfurt, Geneva and Zurich - all European. 
No US city makes both lists. 
— Hong Kong retains the most expensive property, even if prices are down -
10% from pre-Covid levels, but Seoul (3rd) is closing in as tech fever 
envelops a supply constrained market. Zurich, Singapore and Geneva round 
out the top 5. Budapest property prices have tripled (+209%) in 10 years - the 
largest gain in our sample. 
— Japan has completed a remarkable structural repricing: Tokyo's PPP-
implied price level has fallen from 173 in the mid-1990s to just 60 today. 
— Renting a 3-bed apartment in Tokyo is only around a quarter of that in New 
York. A meal for two in Tokyo now costs less than in Warsaw or Prague and is 
a third of the cost of Zurich and New York. A McDonald's costs a quarter of 
that in Tel Aviv and Japan is the cheapest place in the world to buy an iPhone. 
— An iPhone in Türkiye costs 2.2x the US price, the most expensive across 41 
economies we track. South Korea and Japan are the only places where the 
iPhone is cheaper than in the US. 
— US tariffs, by contrast, have left a surprisingly light consumer-level 
footprint - so far. US grocery and car prices are broadly flat YoY . 
— Tel Aviv has experienced a decade of climbing the global prices table and 
now sits at the top of the most expensive city in the world to buy a 
McDonald’s meal. The city is in top 5 for prices of a few items including a 
meal for two, a summer dress and a pair of jeans. 
— Back to 3-bed rentals, and New York retains its top spot, followed by Zurich, 
San Francisco, Boston and Singapore. London (8th), Paris (21st), Frankfurt 
(30th) and Tokyo (40th) are interesting big financial city datapoints. Seoul 
(44th) is much cheaper to rent in than to buy. 
— A monthly public transport pass remains the most expensive in London (by 
a big margin) and remains free in Luxembourg. Sydney, New York, Melbourne 
and Auckland are the other most expensive. Gasoline prices are almost 9 
times as expensive in Hong Kong than Cairo, although a Zurich taxi costs 23 
times that of a Cairo one. 
— German and Central European cities heavily populate the top 10 in terms 
of highest utility bills, a lasting legacy of the energy crisis and the shift away 
from Russian gas and nuclear power. 
— Our “Cheap Date” index sees Geneva, Zurich, Tel Aviv, Oslo and 
Copenhagen edge London out of the top 5 as the most expensive place to be 
a romantic. A date in Geneva costs nearly 5 times that of Delhi.

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank Research Institute 
Mapping the World’s Prices – July 13, 2026

4 
— If you love wine and coffee head to Rome. It has the cheapest bottle of wine 
in the world and the 4th cheapest cappuccino. Lisbon is next cheapest for 
wine while Singapore is the most expensive. Other cheap places for wine 
include Buenos Aires, Budapest, Johannesburg and Cape Town, and even 
Frankfurt. 
— Don’t spill the cheap wine on your summer dress in Buenos Aires as it’s the 
most expensive place to buy a replacement. 
— Access to the internet is the most expensive in the Middle East (Dubai top) 
and India, Russia and China have cheap connectivity.

Methodology: We’ve continued to improve consistency, standardising goods 
and services across geographies and converting all figures into USD. 
Crowdsourced data remains our backbone and thanks to Numbeo for their 
wealth of data here. Numbeo offer data on many more global cities beyond those 
we selected on their website. For our sample, we augment the data by rigorous 
cleaning where necessary across thousands of datapoints. There will always be a 
few puzzling outliers—particularly in the historical numbers—but we’re confident 
this edition offers the most comprehensive snapshot for cities relevant to the 
financial world.

The Macro Backdrop — Currencies, Inflation, and
Purchasing Power
Since this report was first published in 2012 we have seen big swings in inflation,
growth, prices and exchange rates but one of the main enduring trends has been
that the US has become notably more expensive and Japan has travelled from
expensive to very cheap.
Over this period, on a PPP basis, the US has got more expensive relative to every

single one of the 42 economies covered in this report. Australia and Norway have
cheapened up considerably but still rank highly on a PPP basis. The real outlier is
Japan.

Japan's PPP-implied price level has fallen from 173 in the mid-1990s, 125 in 2012,
to just 60 today. A meal for two in Tokyo now costs less than in Warsaw or Prague
and has gone from being around three-quarters of the cost of the meal in New York
in 2012 to only around 30% today in USD terms.

2012 marked the point of maximum Yen strength when after years of funding carry
trades due to ultra-low rates, money flowed back to Japan as other global central
banks also slashed rates close to zero (like Japan had previously for years). The Yen
was also seen as a safe haven in a world of European sovereign stress and
uncertainty about the US post the GFC. Nearly a decade and a half later and Japan
has spent the last few years defying the global round of interest rate normalising

and the Yen has become the premier funding currency of the world again.
Figure 3: USDJPY

Source : Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank.
Given Japanese inflation has been relatively low globally, while its currency has
slumped, Japan now stands out amongst its developed market peers in this report
as being extremely cheap when converting to USDs. The yen is down by -51% since
2012 and inflation is ‘only’ up by +20%. The result is a Tokyo that is now genuinely

cheap by developed-market standards:

n If you account for taxes, Japan is the cheapest place in the world to buy an
iPhone.
n You can have nearly two dates in Tokyo for the price of one in London
according to our “Cheap Date” index.
n A meal out for two in Tokyo is ranked 57th out of 69 and is actually a third
of the cost of the same in Zurich and New York. It’s cheaper than in
Johannesburg, Sao Paulo, Istanbul, Warsaw and Prague amongst dozens

of other global cities on this measure.
n A McDonalds meal now costs four times as much in Tel Aviv (the most
expensive city on this measure) than in does in Tokyo (63rd) with a beer
more almost two times as expensive in London. A cappuccino in Tokyo is
cheaper than in 44 of our 69 focus cities, including EM economies.
n Tokyo property and rental prices, the most expensive in the world around 35
years ago, are now closer to the middle section of our global tables.

n Renting a 3-bedroom apartment in Tokyo (40th) is almost a quarter of the
cost of doing so in New York. In USD terms, it’s down -23 percent since
2016.
n Buying an apartment in Tokyo (25th) is nearly a third of the cost of Asian
rivals Hong Kong and Seoul, and half that of London. Similar to rent costs,
the price per square meter is down by almost a quarter over the last 10 years.
n Salaries have also lagged with Zurich (1st) seeing wages 3.5 times that in

Tokyo (39th), which sits next to Madrid, Prague and Warsaw.

So on virtually all measures, including those in this study, Tokyo and Japan are very
cheap internationally. Note that when we launched this series in 2012, the US was

very cheap versus its peers. For example, New York has moved up +32 places to the
8th most expensive city for a date using our Cheap Date index that includes prices
of a meal for two, coffees, alcohol, taxi and clothes. It is also now ranked 4th for a
fitness membership, jumping +11 ranks since 2012. It is a similar picture for other
US cities that we track.
While economies can stay cheap or expensive for a prolonged period of time, the
AI boom does potentially provide Japan with a potential catalyst for mean reversion

over the medium-term. The Nikkei is up over +70% over the last 12 months and has
finally shaken off the peak level seen at the end of 1989.

While the US leads in frontier model creation, Japan’s opportunity lies in becoming
a leading real-world implementer of AI, especially in industries where it already has
deep manufacturing and robotics expertise.

1.  Demographics as a catalyst. In many Western economies, AI adoption is
often framed through the lens of job displacement. In Japan, the starting
point is different: a shrinking and ageing population has created persistent
labour shortages across sectors, making productivity-enhancing
technologies more economically and politically attractive. This does not
eliminate displacement risk, particularly in white-collar roles, but it means
AI is more likely to be viewed as a tool to ease capacity constraints rather

than simply as a substitute for labour.
2.  A more adoption-oriented policy stance.  Japan’s AI framework is also
relatively supportive of deployment. Unlike the EU AI Act, which
establishes a binding risk-based regulatory regime, Japan’s 2025 AI Act
and subsequent AI Basic Plan are more promotion-oriented, focused on
accelerating research, development and utilisation while addressing risks
through governance, transparency and guidance. The government’s

stated ambition to become “the most AI-friendly country in the world”
underscores this pro-adoption bias.
3.  A potential edge in physical AI. Japan’s industrial base gives it a potential
advantage as AI moves beyond chatbots and software into robotics,
factories, logistics, healthcare and other physical-world applications. The
country has longstanding strengths in industrial robotics, precision
manufacturing and process engineering. So Japan’s opportunity lies in

combining its hardware and manufacturing expertise with faster progress
in AI-enabled systems.
Ultimately, Japan’s back is against the wall demographically. This makes
aggressive AI adoption not just a competitive advantage, but a strict economic
necessity for the country's survival over the next two decades.
So Japan stands out in this study but it’s not the only economy where there have
been big price and FX moves over the last decade or so.

Other Movers in The Last Decade
Exchange rate moves are the single largest driver of year-to-year changes in our
USD-denominated data. The past decade has produced a dramatic divergence:

The Israeli shekel's +30% gain is one of the strongest in our sample and helps
explain why Tel Aviv has climbed so many price rankings. The Czech koruna (+15%)
and Polish zloty (+5%), combined with strong domestic wage growth, and higher
inflation than in Western Europe, have transformed Prague and Warsaw from
bargain cities into mid-price destinations.
At the other extreme, the Argentine peso's near-total collapse (-99%) means
Buenos Aires remains near the bottom of most price tables in USD terms despite

domestic hyperinflation. As already discussed, the yen's -37% decline has turned
Tokyo from one of the world's most expensive cities into a mid-table one.
Israel: Climbing to The Top
You will see in the report that Tel Aviv has seen a big climb up the tables as the
economy gets more expensive in dollar terms partly due to the Shekel being one of
the best performing currencies over the last 12 months (+13%), which keeps it
firmly at the top of the charts over the last decade of major currencies. Strong

domestic tech and defence industries help, as do regional supply disruptions.
Here are some Tel Aviv facts from our study:
n It’s now the most expensive city in the world to buy a McDonald’s meal. The
city is in top 5 for prices of a summer dress and a pair of jeans.
n It also has the second most expensive utility prices in the world, ranking in
between Berlin and Oslo.
n It’s the second most expensive for gasoline, climbing +6 places this year
and +13 over the last decade and second most expensive to buy a car.

n Moves up to 4 th most expensive place for a “meal for two” behind only
Zurich, New York and Geneva. Climbing +3 places this year and +22 over the
last decade.
n Moves up to the third most expensive place for a “cheap date” behind only
Geneva and Zurich. Climbing +2 places this year and +12 over the last
decade.

n Apartment prices are 6th globally with a 3-bed rent 16th.
n Salaries are 12th but up +10 places from last year.
Tel Aviv is no longer a "mid-price" Mediterranean city; it now sits firmly among the
world's most expensive.

Central Europe: The Great Convergence
Alongside Israel, Central Europe represents another example of structural ascent
over the last decade. The numbers are striking:

Five forces have driven this convergence:
1.  EU structural funds boosting investment and productivity
2.  Tight labour markets driving 5–10% annual local-currency wage growth
3.  Currency appreciation (koruna +15%, zloty +5% vs USD over the decade)
4.  Post-Covid inflation (Hungary +57%, Poland +51%, Czech Rep +43%
cumulative since end of 2019) that has not fully reversed
5.  Capital inflows into property
The PPP data confirms the trend: Czech Republic's relative price level has risen from

48 to 63 (vs US = 100), Hungary's from 45 to 57 and Poland's from 42 to 53 over the
past decade. These cities can no longer be considered "cheap" European
destinations. A weekend in Prague or Budapest can now cost more than in many

### Page 11

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 11
Western European cities - a reversal of the dynamic that prevailed for years after the
fall of communism.
Peak US - an Update
Last year we asked whether 2025 might represent "peak US" in pricing terms. One
year on, the evidence is mixed.
Signs the US remains at the top:
n New York retains the #1 spot for rents (1-bed and 3-bed)
n US cities still dominate the top 5 for salaries
n US grocery prices remain among the world's highest

n No visible tariff-driven price acceleration, yet
Signs of potential moderation:
n Several US salary figures were broadly flat
n US car prices broadly flat despite tariffs, suggesting the strong dollar may
be masking underlying pressures
n San Francisco property prices have fallen -15% over the past decade
The stabilisation of US prices, combined with sharp increases in European and
other cities, suggests the gap may be starting to narrow.
Contrast within the US emerges when tracking disposable income which adjusts

for rents. New York is up four places to 39th place this year (-12 places since 2012
as rents have soared), and although Boston (5th) has slipped a place in 2026, it is up
+25 places since 2012. San Francisco is 3rd in 2026, up 35 places in a decade. So
despite the overall price increases and big affordability issues in New York in
particular, a few other big US cities have been doing well as salaries have kept up
with housing affordability.
The Post-Covid Inflation Scorecard

Inflation was always with us in various parts of the world in the 2010s when this
report first started but for many parts, especially developed markets it was largely
subdued. However since Covid global inflation is well and truly back and is shaping
relative prices and currencies everywhere. Cumulative CPI inflation since 2019
reveals where the cost of living has risen most in local currency terms — stripping
out FX effects:

Quality of Life
The quality-of-life index combines purchasing power, safety, healthcare, cost of
living, property-price-to-income ratio, commute time, pollution, and climate into a
single score.
Figure 10: Quality of Life Top 10 (2026) vs 2025 edition
Current rank City Rank change
from 2025
1. Luxembourg +0
2. Copenhagen +0
3. Amsterdam +0
4. Vienna -1
4. Munich +8
6. Frankfurt +1
7. Helsinki -2

8. Geneva -2
9. Zurich -1
10. Edinburgh +0
Source : www.numbeo.com, Deutsche Bank.
Luxembourg, Copenhagen, Amsterdam and Vienna keep their places at the top
from last year. This is despite affordability pressures as both Vienna and
Copenhagen have seen their property to income ratio ranks deteriorate by -13 and
-9 positions from last year's edition, respectively. Still, low traffic commute times
and pollution help keep those cities at the top.
Frankfurt now ranks just outside the top 5. Germany's financial capital has

improved its cost-of-living rank (from 50th to 42nd) while maintaining very short
commute times (1st globally) and low pollution. It now ranks above both Geneva
and Zurich where cost of living is bottom of our 69-city pile. Another German city
to enter top 10 this year is Munich (+8 ranks) amidst stronger purchasing power
score. It now ties with Vienna on the fourth place for the overall index.
Hong Kong has fallen outside our top-50 list and now ranks 55th, down from 48th

place in last year's edition. It is up just +2 places since 2019. Extremely high costs,
property prices ranked 68th worst, pollution and long commutes all weigh on its
score.
London continues to hover near the bottom of our top-50 table but has moved up
+3 places since last year, with Birmingham also up by +7 to now sit in the middle
of the table at 25th place.
Notably, no US city makes the top 10 for quality of life but there is much dispersion.

New York at 46th and Los Angeles at 49th are towards the lower-end of the table,
while Boston (17th) and San Francisco is (21st) are comfortably in the top half. The
combination of high costs, long commutes, and safety concerns keeps American
cities out of contention for the top ten though - despite their salary dominance.

US salary growth has been dispersed, with San Francisco (+74%) and Boston

As with property prices, the big growth has been happening in EM cities over the

last decade. Seven of the top 10 cities on this measure have seen rent prices more
than double. A few European cities, including Lisbon, Amsterdam and Dublin, have
seen strong rent growth, driven by supply constraints, the return of tourism and
remote-work migration.
Some of the highlights on the other end of the spectrum are Tokyo, Beijing,
Shanghai, San Francisco and Seoul, which have all seen declines over the decade.
Dubai, London and Hong Kong have seen some of the smallest positive growth

rates in our sample.

### Page 21

The Cost of Living
Utilities
The highlight is Tel Aviv's surge to 2nd place, up +10 ranks from 2025, amidst
energy markets disruptions in the region. German cities continue to dominate the
top ranks, with Berlin 1st, Munich 6th and Frankfurt 7th, a lasting legacy of the
energy crisis and the shift away from Russian gas and nuclear power. Oslo, London
and Edinburgh complete the top 5.Cheapest utilities remain in Egypt, India and

China.
Groceries
The Numbeo groceries index reflects the cost of groceries in a city relative to New
York (=100).  Only Zurich, Geneva and San Francisco are more expensive on this
measure than New York across our sample cities. The remaining US cities are all in
top 10 on this measure, with Boston 5th, Los Angeles 7th and Chicago 8th . Oslo, Tel
Aviv (+8 ranks from last year) and Seoul complete the top 10.

Since the index we use is calculated relative to New York, its changes are useful as
a guide of how expensive our sample cities have become relative to US. Over the last
decade, Warsaw, Prague and Budapest have stood out on this measure but many

other European cities have also become more expensive. Meanwhile, many Asian
cities, beyond Tokyo which has been a spotlight throughout this report, have
cheapened on a relative basis.
Since 2012, when this report was first published, the change is more dramatic. The
US was a much cheaper place and since then only 10 of our sample cities have seen
their groceries price indices increase relative to New York, three of them are in the
US.

Note: We have defined a cheap date as follows: 1 bottle of wine, 1 pair of jeans, 1 dress, 2 coffees, 1 meal for 2 people in mid range restaurant, 2 cinema tickets, 2 transport tickets one way, 5km taxi trip
Over the last decade, dating has become more expensive not only in Emerging

markets with Budapest and Mexico City at the top, but also in the US, with Los
Angeles, New York and Boston in the top 10 based on the cost increase. The
cheapening of Asia is clear in this index too, with 8 out of top 10 cities by decade-
long declines located in Asia with Shanghai the most improved on cost.
If you're looking for romance on a budget, India remains the absolute cheapest
answer though: Delhi ($103), Bangalore ($106), and Mumbai ($115) occupy the

bottom three spots and have also seen dating costs decline over the last decade.
The Oasis Index (Cigarettes & Beer)
Melbourne ($111.2) is the world's most expensive city for cigarettes and beer,
continuing Australia's relentless application of sin taxes, with Sydney 2nd. Over a
decade, Melbourne's Oasis index has risen +89%. Auckland and Wellington rank
3rd and 4th respectively.
The UK also ranks near the top - London (5th, $63.9), Birmingham (6th, $62.8), and

Edinburgh (8th, $60.5) - reflecting high tobacco duties. Dublin, Oslo and New York
close out the top 10. Cheapest: Shanghai ($10.4), Rio ($11.7) and Cairo ($12.1).
Wine and Coffee

### Page 25

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 25
Wine: Singapore ($23.2) remains the most expensive place to buy a bottle of mid-
range wine, followed by Jakarta ($21.1). The cheapest? Rome ($4.6) and Lisbon
($5.7).
Cappuccino: Zurich ($7.13) reclaims the top spot, followed by Copenhagen ($6.96)
and Geneva ($6.41). Italian cities remain reassuringly cheap: a cappuccino in Milan
costs just $2.18 - less than a third of the Zurich price. Three of US cities - New York,

Los Angeles and San Francisco - are in top 10. Dubai, Tel Aviv and Doha are also in
the list.
Similar to eating out, having a coffee has become more expensive almost
everywhere except for a few big Asian cities, with only 6 seeing 10-year declines.
Central European cities are ahead once again.

Gasoline
Hong Kong ($3.88/litre) remains the most expensive - 8.6x the price in Cairo ($0.45)

The iPhone Index
The iPhone has become one of our most reliable cross-economy price
comparisons. Apple sets prices centrally and the product is identical worldwide —
so price differences reflect taxes, tariffs, distribution costs, and currency effects,
making it an excellent barometer of trade and fiscal policy.
Figure 27: Prices of iPhone 17 Pro (256GB) and iPhone 7 from the 2017 Mapping the World's Prices edition, full available

list (USD)
0
500
1,000
1,500
2,000
2,500
3,000 iPhone 17 Pro 2026 iPhone 7 2017
Source : Official Apple Website, Online Retailers, Deutsche Bank.
Note: Prices are for iPhone 17 Pro (256GB). The prices are sourced from official Apple store websites. In the absence of official prices via websites, we have used prices from online retailers. As of mid-June 2026.
Key observations:
n Japan ($1,121, -5% vs US) offers the cheapest iPhone amidst yen
weakness.

n South Korea (2nd cheapest) remains among the cheapest globally, amidst
domestic competition from Samsung.
n Türkiye at 2.4x the US price reflects high taxes and the lira's -92% collapse
over the decade.
n Brazil (2.1x) also shows the impact of taxes designed to encourage local
assembly.
n Taxes also raise the relative price of an iPhone in Hungary (27% VAT).
n Switzerland ($1,385) is the cheapest European economy in our sample
despite being the world's most expensive economy for nearly everything

else - thanks to its low 8.1% VAT.
n Hong Kong (0% VAT, +9% premium) serves as a useful benchmark for
Apple's base distribution margin outside the US.

### Page 30

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Page 30 Deutsche Bank AG
Conclusion
If there is one overarching lesson from this tenth edition of Mapping the World's
Prices, it is that the global cost-of-living map is being redrawn less by one single
shock than by the cumulative force of currencies, inflation, housing constraints,
geopolitics and long-running structural change. In dollar terms, the world now
looks very different from 2012, from 2016, and even from the pre-Covid benchmark

of 2019. The old assumptions about which places are expensive, which are cheap,
and where wages compensate for living costs no longer always hold.
The most dramatic structural story remains Japan. Tokyo, once synonymous with
extreme expense, now sits in the middle or lower half of many developed-market
price tables once converted into dollars. A weak yen, decades of low inflation and
subdued wage growth have turned Japan into one of the clearest examples in this

report of how relative prices can move over very long cycles. The irony is that this
cheapness may now be part of Japan’s opportunity set. If AI adoption, automation
and the country’s demographic urgency combine to lift productivity, today’s
apparent undervaluation could become one of the more interesting mean-reversion
stories of the next decade.
At the other end of the spectrum, Tel Aviv has moved from being an expensive city
to being one of the defining outliers of this year’s survey. The strength of the shekel,

the resilience of Israel’s tech and defence sectors, regional shortages and the
impact of conflict have combined to push it up a striking number of tables. It is now
among the most expensive cities in the world for everything from meals out and
utilities to cars, clothing, gym memberships and even McDonald’s. Tel Aviv’s
repricing has been unusually broad-based.
Central Europe provides the other major convergence story. Prague, Warsaw and
Budapest are no longer the obvious bargains they once were. Strong wage growth,

EU-related investment, tighter labour markets, higher post-Covid inflation and
capital inflows into property have all pushed these cities closer to Western
European price levels. In some areas, particularly housing and leisure, the
convergence has been startling. That is good news in the sense that it reflects
genuine economic progress, but less welcome for anyone still hoping for a cheap
weekend in Prague.
The US story is more nuanced. American cities still dominate several income and

rent tables, and New York remains the global rental benchmark by some distance.
Yet the evidence for a new tariff-driven consumer price shock is limited so far.
Groceries and car prices in the US are not yet flashing red, even if lag effects, retailer
margin absorption or dollar strength may be masking pressures that could appear
later.
Housing remains the thread that runs through almost every liveability discussion.
High salaries alone do not guarantee a comfortable life if rent consumes the gains.

This is why cities such as Luxembourg, Copenhagen, Zurich, Geneva and Frankfurt
stand out: they combine high quality-of-life scores with strong disposable income
after housing costs. By contrast, New York, London, Paris and Hong Kong remain
global magnets for talent and capital, but their liveability scores are weighed down
by expensive housing, long commutes, pollution or affordability pressures. The
lesson is that the richest cities are not always the easiest places to live.

The more playful indices tell the same story in miniature. A cheap date is no longer

### Page 31

13 July 2026
Thematic Research
Deutsche Bank AG Page 31
especially cheap in much of Europe. London remains the world leader in expensive
public transport. Rome is still cheap for wine and coffee. Singapore remains in a
category of its own for cars. Türkiye and Brazil show how taxes, tariffs and currency
weakness can distort the price of an iPhone. And, as ever, Switzerland somehow
manages to be expensive in nearly everything while still preserving exceptional

purchasing power.
Looking ahead, the key question is not simply which city tops which table in 2026,
but which of these shifts proves cyclical and which proves structural. The strong
dollar, the weak yen, the expensive shekel, the post-Covid inflation legacy, the
European housing squeeze, the tariff shock and the AI investment cycle will not all
move in the same direction forever. If history is any guide, the next few years will
contain as many reversals as continuations.

For now, this year’s report shows a world where Switzerland remains expensive,
Japan has become very cheap, Tel Aviv has surged, Central Europe has converged,
the US remains powerful but perhaps no longer accelerating, and quality of life
increasingly depends on whether income can survive the rent bill. As always, these
are highlights from a much larger dataset. Readers should focus less on any single
rank and more on the direction of travel — because it is the direction, not the precise

decimal point, that tells the real story.

Note: Index ranks are based on indices published periodically on source website. Rank changes are affected by many cities missing 2012 data.
Note on methodology: The Numbeo Quality-of-Life index is an empirical measure

of the living standards across various cities. The index is calculated by aggregating
various sub-indices that measure certain broad factors that affect overall quality of
life in a given location. These underlying sub-indices measure (1) consumer
purchasing power (purchasing power index); (2) regional crime and safety (safety
index); (3) overall quality of health care availability (healthcare index); (4) general
cost of consumer goods (cost of living index); (5) housing affordability (property

price to income ratio); (6) traffic congestion and commute times (traffic commute
time index); (7) overall pollution (pollution index); (8) regional climate likeability
(climate index). Further details on methodology around data collection and
aggregation can be found at the source website here.

## Document 323: OPEC有志７ヵ国､８月も６月･７月と同じ日量18.8万バレル増産で合意 ～結束の維持を重視も､市場では過剰供給への警戒感が高まる可能性～

Source PDF: all report pdf/OPEC有志７ヵ国､８月も６月･７月と同じ日量18.8万バレル増産で合意 ～結束の維持を重視も､市場では過剰供給への警戒感が高まる可能性～.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年 7 月 6 日

1/3 
OPEC 有志７ヵ国､８月も６月･７月と同じ日量 18.8 万バレル増産で合意 
～結束の維持を重視も､市場では過剰供給への警戒感が高まる可能性～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国は７月５日のオンライン閣僚会合で、８月も日量18.8 万バレルの
増産を実施することで合意した。ＵＡＥがOPEC を脱退したことでOPEC プラスの世界原油生
産に占めるシェアや余剰生産能力は低下しており、需給調整能力の低下が懸念されている。
それでも、有志７ヵ国は６月、７月に続いて小幅増産を容認することで、協調体制の維持と
価格形成への影響力確保を図る姿勢を示した。 
○ 一方、４月以降に生産枠は引き上げられているものの、中東情勢の緊迫化やホルムズ海峡の
事実上の封鎖により、湾岸産油国の実際の産油量は減少しており、増産は名目上のものにと
どまっている。６月以降はＵＡＥの増産や米国による輸出支援を背景に生産量は回復しつつ
あるが、なお中東情勢緊迫化前の水準を下回っている。にもかかわらず、原油価格は中国の
輸入減少、中東以外の産油国による輸出拡大、ＩＥＡ主導の戦略備蓄放出などを受け、戦争
前の水準を下回るまで落ち着きを取り戻している。 
○ 先行きについては、 有志７ヵ国が９月も同規模の増産を続ければ、2023 年に合意した自主減
産枠は９月末までに解消される見込みである。 一方、OPEC プラス全体では協調減産の維持を
掲げているものの、ＵＡＥの脱退やイラクによる生産枠引き上げ要求など、枠組み内の結束
に綻びが生じるリスクが残る。各国の財政均衡原油価格に差があるなか、原油価格が一定水
準を上回る局面では増産圧力が強まりやすく、金融市場では原油の過剰供給への警戒感が高
まる可能性がある。

【有志７ヵ国は６月、７月に続き、８月も日量18.8 万バレルの増産で合意】 
主要産油国の枠組みである OPEC プラスの有志７ヵ国（サウジアラビア、ロシア、イラク、クウ
ェート、カザフスタン、アルジェリア、オマーン）は、７月５日にオンラインで閣僚会合を開催し
た。有志国会合を巡っては、 その一角を担っていたＵＡＥ （アラブ首長国連邦）が５月１日付でOPEC
（石油輸出国機構）を脱退しており、今回は同国を除く７ヵ国体制による３度目の会合となる。 
中東情勢の緊迫化を受けて原油価格が急上昇したため、 有志８ヵ国は４月、 ５月と２ヵ月連続で
日量 20.6 万バレルの増産に合意した。しかし、前述の通りＵＡＥがOPEC から脱退し、世界の原油

### Page 2

2/3 
生産量に占めるOPEC プラスのシェアは45％程度と、それ以前に比べて３pt 低下している。 さらに、
OPEC プラス全体としての余剰生産能力も約２割低下しており、需給調整能力の低下が懸念された。 
こうしたなか、ＵＡＥを除く有志７ヵ国は、６月、７月と、ＵＡＥの増産分を除いた分に相当す
る日量 18.8 万バレルの増産を実施してきた。８月についても、過去２ヵ月と同じ日量18.8 万バレ
ルの増産で合意し、OPEC プラスとしての協調体制の維持をあらためて確認した。世界需要の0.2％
程度に相当する小幅増産を容認する一方で、 枠内の盟主であるサウジアラビアが主導する生産調整
を通じて価格形成面での影響力を維持したい思惑がうかがえる。

【供給の混乱は続いているが､足元の原油価格は戦争前の水準まで低下】 
前述の通り、有志国は４月以降に生産枠を日量約 80 万バレル引き上げてきた。しかし、中東情
勢の緊迫化を受けたホルムズ海峡の事実上の封鎖を受けて、 サウジアラビアやクウェート、 イラク
といった湾岸産油国の産油量は減少しており、 実態としては名目上のものにとどまっている。OPEC
が公表した最新のデータによれば、OPEC プラス全体の産油量は２月の日量4,277 万バレルから、 ５
月には同3,313 万バレルに減少した。 ６月以降は、OPEC から脱退したＵＡＥによる増産のほか、 米
国による OPEC プラスの国々の輸出を後押しする取り組みも追い風に生産量は回復し始めているも
のの、その水準は中東情勢の緊迫化前を下回っている。さらに、米国とイランによる停戦合意以降
も、ホルムズ海峡を通過する船舶の数は戦争前の半分程度にとどまる。 
このように供給の混乱が続いているに
もかかわらず、足元の原油価格は戦争前
の水準 を下回るまで 落ち着きを取り戻
している（図１）。 中国による輸入の減少
に加え、中東以外の産油国による輸出拡
大、ＩＥＡ（国際エネルギー機関）が調
整した過去最大規模の世界戦略備蓄の
放出の動きも相場の重しとなっている。
今回の有志国会合で の増産決定を受け
て、当面の原油価格は上値の重い展開が
続く可能性は高まっている。当面の原油
価格については、引き続きホルムズ海峡を巡る情勢に左右される展開が続くと見込まれる。

【OPECプラスに綻びのリスク､市場には過剰供給を懸念する向きも】 
先行きは、仮に有志７ヵ国が９月も日量 18.8 万バレルの増産を継続した場合、2023 年に有志国
が合意した日量165 万バレルの自主減産枠は９月末までに解消される。一方、OPEC プラス全体とし
ては、2026 年末まで実施される枠組み全体としての協調減産を維持する方針を示すなど、 枠組みの
結束をあらためて演出することを重視した。しかし、ＵＡＥによる OPEC 脱退と増産を受けて、枠
内の結束にほころびが生じるリスクは残る。報道によれば、イラクが OPEC による生産枠の大幅引
図 1 原油価格の推移

（出所）LSEG より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

### Page 3

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
3/3
き上げを要望しており、 仮に受け入れられなければ脱退を検討していることが明らかになっている。
こうした動きが出ている背景には、 加盟国間における原油価格の財政均衡水準が異なることが影
響している。足元の原油価格は多くの国で財政均衡水準を上回っていることから、各国が増産に動
く誘因は高まっている。前述の通り、 足元の原油価格は戦争前の水準を下回る水準となるなど落ち
着きを取り戻しているなか、 仮に増産に向けた圧力が一段と強まれば、 金融市場においては原油の
過剰供給を懸念する向きが強まることも予想される。

## Document 485: 内外経済ウォッチ『米国～FRBのフォワードガイダンス排除による不確実性の高まり～』(2026年8月号)

Source PDF: all report pdf/内外経済ウォッチ『米国～FRBのフォワードガイダンス排除による不確実性の高まり～』(2026年8月号).pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-07-21
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国
～ FRBのフォワードガイダンス排除による不確実性の高まり～
ケビン ・ ウォーシュ氏は、 2026年5月22日にホワイ トハ
ウスで行われた就任宣誓式を経て、 FRBの第17代議長に
就任した。 新議長のもとで初開催となった6月16、 17日
のFOMCにおいて、 FRBはFF金利の誘導目標レンジを
3.50～3.75％で据え置くことを全会一致で決定した。 4
会合連続の据え置きは市場の事前予想通りであり、 潤沢
な準備預金枠を維持するバランスシー ト政策の方針にも
変更はなかった。
しかし、 同時に公表された経済 ・ 金利予測 （SEP） では、
年内の利下げ予想がわずか1名にとどまった一方、 利上
げ予想が増加し、 FRBが明確にタカ派へシフ ト したことが
浮き彫りとなった。 声明文でもインフレ目標達成への強
いコミ ッ トメン トが示され、 金融市場では （7月8日時点で）
年内の利上げを織り込む動きが進んでいる。
ウォーシュ氏は以前からFRBの構造改革を主張してお
り、 新議長主導の下、 FRBのコミュニケーシ ョ ンスタイル

には劇的な変化がもたらされている。 議長は 「声明文は判
断できる限りの事実を淡々と伝えるものであるべき」 との
持論に基づき表現を大幅に簡素化。 従来のフォワー ドガ
イダンスを声明文から完全に排除した。
これまで政策指針となっていた 「追加調整の程度と時
期を検討する」 という文言の削除により、 利下げバイアス
は完全に払拭された。 議長はFOMC後の記者会見で 「現
在の政策局面には適していないと委員会が一致した」 と、
これがFOMCの総意であることを強調した。 さらにリスク
評価や政策変更の条件に関する記述も削除され、 先行き
への思惑を誘う文言が一掃された。 議長は会見でも今後
の市場への約束を拒否し、 先行きのガイダンスを明確に
拒む姿勢を示している。
また、 会見冒頭の大半はFRB改革の表明に費やされ
ウォーシュ体制の始動とFOMCのタカ派シフ ト
フォワードガイダンスの一掃とFRBの構造改革
た。 議長は政策運営の中核となる以下の5つの領域にお
いて、 それぞれタスクフォースを立ち上げる方針を発表し

た。 これらは年内に結果が報告される予定であり、 今後の
政策運営に大きな影響を与えるとみられる。 ①コミュニ
ケーシ ョ ン ： SEPの開示方法を抜本的に見直す。 ②バラン
スシー ト政策 ： 潤沢な準備預金制度の利害と資産構成の
再検証を進める。 ③データソースの刷新 ： 情報源や収集手
法を刷新し、 実用的な経済情報を提供する。 ④新技術の
影響把握 ： 生成AI等の普及が労働市場や二大責務 （雇用
の最大化と物価の安定） に与えるインパク トを調査する。
⑤インフレ枠組みの再検証 ： インフレ駆動要因を再検証
し、 物価安定のアイデアを比較検討する。
外部環境では、 米国とイランが戦闘終結で合意したも
のの、 最終合意への道筋は不透明であり、 ホルムズ海峡
を巡る地政学リスクも燻る。 しかし、 原油価格は既に下落
に転じており、 6月以降の米インフレ鈍化が見込まれる。
こう した中、 7月1日の講演でウォーシュ議長は 「この4
週間でインフレ期待は低下しており、 インフレのリスク自

体は減少した」 と言及し、 これまでの強い警戒感をやや
トーンダウンさせた。 一方、 景気認識に関しては 「労働市
場は依然と して安定しており、 成長見通しも改善している
可能性がある」 と指摘した。 雇用環境の堅調さが維持され
る中で、 米経済の実質成長率が上振れする可能性を示唆
している。
ウォーシュ議長の改革により、 市場が長年頼りにしてき
た 「先行きのヒン ト」 は完全に失われた。 今後は中央銀行
による丁寧な対話がない、 真のデータディペンデン ト （経
済指標次第） の局面へと移行する。 結果と して、 今後は主
要な経済指標が発表されるたびに市場が一喜一憂する
展開となり、 金融市場全体のボラティ リティが大きく高ま
る可能性が高い。
データ次第の政策見通しと市場への影響
経済調査部　主任エコノミス ト　桂畑 誠治（かつらはた せいじ）
6第一ライフ研レポー ト 2026.08

## Document 517: 剧烈反弹！美股“科技动量股”创史上最大单日涨幅，但暴跌结束了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/剧烈反弹！美股“科技动量股”创史上最大单日涨幅，但暴跌结束了吗？.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

美股科技动量股周⼆创下史上最⼤单⽇涨幅，摩根⼠丹利 TMT 动量因⼦单⽇涨超 12% ，⾼
盛⾼⻉塔动量指数涨约 8.5% 。⾼盛和瑞银认为动量抛售接近尾声，建议逐步加仓；但 BTIG
警告反弹已触及关键阻⼒区间，市场⼴度偏弱，建议逢⾼减仓。债券收益率同步⾛⾼，财
报季成为下⼀个关键变量。
摘要：
美股科技动量股周⼆（ 7 ⽉ 21 ⽇）上演剧烈反弹。摩根⼠丹利 TMT 动量因⼦单⽇涨幅超过 12% ，
创下有记录以来最⼤单⽇涨幅，甚⾄超过 2000 年互联⽹泡沫时期的任何单⽇表现。⾼盛⾼⻉塔
动量多头指数（ GSCBHMOM ）单⽇上涨约 8.5% ，为 2025 年 4 ⽉以来最强单⽇表现；多空⾼⻉
塔动量指数（ GSPRHIMO ）更是上涨 9.5% ，为 2021 年以来最强，接近 2003 年以来的历史最⾼
⽔平。
纳斯达克综合指数当⽇上涨约 1.3% ，领跑三⼤指数。半导体板块是最⼤推⼿ —— 费城半导体指
数单⽇涨幅达 4.6%， VanEck 半导体 ETF 上涨约 4.5% 。美光科技涨超 10% ，英特尔涨约 8.6% ，

闪迪涨约 14% ， Cerebras Systems 涨约 18% ， Cipher Mining 涨超 11% 。
这场反弹发⽣在此前连续三个交易⽇下跌之后，也发⽣在动量股累计暴跌 33% 之后。
为什么会有这场反弹？空头被 “ 逼仓 ”
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

理解这次反弹，需要先理解此前的跌幅有多深。
⾼盛数据显⽰，⾼⻉塔动量股在短短数个交易⽇内累计下跌 33% ，是互联⽹泡沫破裂以来最惨
烈的回撤之⼀。⾼盛⾼⻉塔动量指数⼀度跌破 200 ⽇均线，触及今年 1 ⽉以来最低点，超卖程度
为去年 8 ⽉以来最严重。
跌得越深，反弹的弹性越⼤ —— 这是市场的基本逻辑。
本次反弹在很⼤程度上是⼀场 " 逼仓 " ⾏情。⼤量做空动量股的投资者，尤其是韩国和⽇本的追涨
杀跌型交易者，在过去两周遭受重创 —— 韩国市场甚⾄出现⼤规模追加保证⾦（ margin call ）事
件，重创当地散⼾群体。当这些空头被迫平仓回补，买盘就会形成⾃我强化的上涨螺旋。
Zacks Investment Research 分析指出，美光科技此前跌破了⽇线图上的 " 头肩顶 " 颈线，技术形
态看空。但周⼆股价暴涨逾 10% ，重新站回颈线之上。 " 假突破往往引发反向的剧烈波动，因为
后知后觉的空头和做空者被困住了。 "
市场⼴度依然偏弱，反弹成⾊存疑
反弹的数字看起来惊⼈，但内部结构并不健康。

BTIG 策略师 Jonathan Krinsky 分析，周⼆整体成交量偏低， SPY 、 QQQ 和标普 500 现货成交量
均⽐ 20 ⽇均值低 20% ⾄ 30% 。与此同时，标普 500 指数当⽇上涨近 1% ，但下跌个股数量仍多于
上涨个股 —— 这是今年以来价格与市场⼴度背离次数最多的⼀年，⽽周⼆再次出现这⼀现象。

### Page 3

⾼盛交易员数据显⽰，交易所整体成交量较 20 ⽇均值低约 17% ，做市商账⾯流动性仅为 683 万美
元，市场活跃度在满分 10 分中仅为 3 分。
换句话说，这场反弹更像是少数重仓股的集中爆发，⽽⾮全⾯复苏。
彭博宏观策略师 Michael Ball 分析， " 现在宣布调整结束还为时过早 " 。半导体 ETF 和前期 AI 明星
股的看跌期权需求依然⾼企，纳斯达克、半导体 ETF 及相关个股的负 Gamma 敞⼝，意味着做市
商会追涨杀跌⽽⾮平抑波动 —— 这在放⼤上涨的同时，也会放⼤下跌。
BTIG 警告：反弹已触及关键阻⼒，建议逢⾼减仓
并⾮所有⼈都对这次反弹感到乐观。
BTIG 的 Jonathan Krinsky 明确警告，建议 " 逢⾼减仓（ fade ） " 。他此前预判动量股反弹会遭遇
730 ⾄ 750 区间的强阻⼒，⽽周⼆的反弹恰好将 GSCBHMOM 推⾄该阻⼒区间下沿。
Krinsky 表⽰： "极端的波动性，加上历史性的个股分化，是市场正在经历⼀轮全⾯回调的信

号。 " 他预计⾼⻉塔动量股将在周三⾄周四进⼊阻⼒区间核⼼后开始滞涨。
从历史数据看，⾃ 1999 年以来，⾼⻉塔动量多头指数在 200 ⽇均线上⽅单⽇涨幅超过 7% 的情况
仅出现过 10 次。其中三次发⽣在今年，三次发⽣在 2021 年初，三次发⽣在 2000 年初。 Krinsky 指
出，这⼀数据 " 既说明了此次⾏情的罕⻅性，也说明我们持续看到与 1999 ⾄ 2000 年时期相呼应的
统计特征 " 。

### Page 4

⾼盛、瑞银：动量抛售接近尾声，建议逐步加仓
与 BTIG 的谨慎态度不同，⾼盛和瑞银均认为此次动量抛售已进⼊尾声，并建议投资者把握机
会。
⾼盛的 Julia Mensch 在报告中指出，⾼盛上周已提⽰动量抛售 " 进⼊后期 " 。她写道：" 随着持仓
已⼤幅出清（⾼盛主经纪商数据显⽰，动量敞⼝处于过去⼀年的第 64 百分位，过去五年的第 93
百分位），且此次抛售背后并⽆新的基本⾯催化剂，我们认为动量有空间向⻓期趋势回归，此次
抛售可能是增加动量敞⼝或逢低买⼊ AI 股的好机会。 "
瑞银对冲基⾦股票衍⽣品销售主管 Michael Romano 也在客⼾报告中表达了类似看法，认为 AI 基
本⾯改善是买⼊信号。但他同时建议投资者 " 逐步建仓，⽽⾮⼀次性全仓杀⼊ " 。
Romano 写道： " 动量去⻛险化是且仍然是⼀个有说服⼒的判断。分批建仓是审慎之举。 " 他预计
动量抛售将在 7 ⽉底前触底（如果尚未触底的话），并表⽰： " ⼀旦⾏情转向，我预计流动性将推
动价格向上超调。 "

不过，⾼盛也保持了⼀定保留态度 —— 鉴于近期波动率极⾼，且财报季密集来袭，⾼盛建议投
资者通过 " 有限损失结构 " （ limited-loss structures ）来获取敞⼝，⽽⾮直接持有多头。
财报季是下⼀个关键变量
这场反弹的持续性，很⼤程度上取决于本周财报。
据路透社报道，本周共有 113 家标普 500 成分股公司（占标普 500 市值约 18% ）发布财报。其
中， Alphabet （ GOOGL ）的财报被视为 " 本周最重要的数据点 "，市场将重点关注其 2026 年全
年资本开⽀指引 —— 普遍预期将上调，这将为 AI ⽀出的⾛向提供重要线索。
LPL Financial ⾸席技术策略师 Adam Turnquist 表⽰： " 现在的焦点不只是资本开⽀总量，下⼀个
关注点将是投资回报率和⽀出质量，我们认为这将在下半年成为核⼼议题。 "
他同时指出： " 我们预计半导体板块将继续出现波动，因为超买状态需要消化，获利了结压⼒将
出现，拥挤的持仓也需要出清。从基本⾯来看，我们认为并没有什么实质性变化。 "

据路透社报道，截⾄⽬前，已有 66 家标普 500 公司公布财报，其中约 88% 的公司盈利超出分析师
预期。 3M （ MMM ）单⽇涨超 9% ，通⽤汽⻋（ GM ）涨约 5% ，均因业绩超预期⽽受到追捧。
债券与宏观：另⼀个隐患

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

股市狂欢的同时，债券市场却在发出警告。
当⽇美债收益率全线⾛⾼，短端 2 年期收益率上涨 5 个基点， 30 年期上涨 2 个基点，⻓端收益率升
⾄两个⽉⾼位，抹去了上周低于预期的通胀数据带来的债券涨幅。
油价是推⼿之⼀。布伦特原油期货收盘重新站上每桶 90 美元，为 6 ⽉ 11 ⽇以来⾸次。中东局势持
续升温 —— 也⻔胡塞武装宣布封锁红海南部⼊⼝，两艘运载沙特原油的油轮在红海掉头折返。
Kpler 的 MarineTraffic 数据显⽰，即便在封锁宣布之前，过去两周通过曼德海峡的货物装载量已
下降 34% 。
RBC Capital Markets 利率策略师 Izaac Brook 表⽰： " 今天的市场⾛势，主要是能源价格持续上
涨的结果。利率的波动因突破了备受关注的技术位 ——2 年期收益率 4.20% 和 10 年期收益率

4.60%—— 以及夏季典型的低流动性交易环境⽽被放⼤。 "
彭博的 Cameron Crise 则警告，⻓债收益率正处于将 5% 从阻⼒位变为⽀撑位的临界点，下⼀个
明显⽬标位是 5.5%——" 这将对股市造成冲击，尤其是当经济上⾏超预期推动收益率⾛⾼，并对
股票产⽣负⾯影响时 " 。
⾼盛 IG 信⽤主管 Kevin Boova 也警告，超⼤规模科技公司的信⽤利差已创新⾼， " 超⼤规模云计
算 /AI/ 数据中⼼领域感觉再度有些脆弱 " 。

## Document 606: 无视美元走强，黄金强势站上4100美元，反转信号出现了吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/无视美元走强，黄金强势站上4100美元，反转信号出现了吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-22
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

汇丰认为市场已消化紧缩预期，⾦价或缓步⾛⾼，但地缘政治引发油价飙升是最⼤下⾏⻛
险；摩根⼤通则指出， 4197—4264 美元阻⼒未破，中期偏空格局未变，此轮上涨更像技术
性修复⽽⾮趋势反转。中期阻⼒位于 4500 美元附近。若⾦价重新加速下⾏，更下⽅⽀撑或
指向 3600 美元区域附近。
摘要：
⻩⾦展现出罕⻅的抗压韧性，在美元⾛强、收益率上⾏、油价⾼企的三重逆⻛下仍强势上涨。
然⽽，汇丰与摩根⼤通的最新研报均指向同⼀结论：此轮上涨更像是超跌后的技术性修复，⽽
⾮趋势性反转，上⾏空间依然有限。
此轮⾦价上涨发端于周⼆的亚洲及伦敦交易时段，随后延续⾄美国市场，并在周三亚市突破
4100 美元关⼝，⽇内最⾼升⾄ 4141.11 美元，全程缺乏明确的基本⾯驱动。
据汇丰贵⾦属⾸席分析师 James Steel 在 7 ⽉ 21 ⽇的研报中分析，上海⻩⾦交易所溢价升⾄ 6 ⾄ 8
美元 / 盎司，显⽰国内实物需求有所回暖，⼀笔⼤额集中买单可能是触发此轮涨势的直接导⽕
索。与此同时，美国私营部⻔就业数据（ ADP ）连续第四周下滑⾄净新增 1.65 万个职位，但这

⼀疲软信号对⾦价的提振作⽤同样难以量化。
该⾏还认为，市场已在相当程度上消化了货币政策收紧预期，⾦价有望继续缓步⾛⾼，但地缘
政治⻛险 —— 尤其是中东局势升级与油价进⼀步上涨 —— 仍是最⼤威胁。
摩根⼤通技术策略师 Jason Hunter 则从图表层⾯泼下冷⽔。他指出，⻩⾦⽬前仅是在 4074 美元
（ 2022 年 8 ⽉ 38.2% 斐波那契回撤位）附近寻求⽀撑，动量背离买⼊信号的出现暗⽰市场可能进
⼊更⻓时间的盘整，但在 4197 ⾄ 4264 美元的趋势线阻⼒群被有效突破之前，⾦价的中期偏空格
局并未改变。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

三重逆⻛下的异常上涨
此轮⾦价上涨之所以引发市场关注，在于其发⽣的宏观背景极为不利。美元指数⾛强、美债收
益率上⾏、油价维持⾼位 —— 这三项因素历来是压制⻩⾦的核⼼变量，⽽⾦价却在三者同步施
压之下逆势⾛⾼。
汇丰的 James Steel 将这⼀现象解读为潜在内⽣强势的信号。他在报告中写道，⻩⾦在 4000 美元
附近⻓时间横盘整理后，投资者判断向上突破的时机已经成熟，此次上涨或许不仅仅是简单的
技术性反弹，市场可能已在相当程度上消化了货币政策收紧的预期，其他⻛险因素则对⾦价构
成⽀撑。
不过， James Steel 同时强调，这并不意味着上⾏通道已经畅通⽆阻。他认为，若地缘政治局势
进⼀步升级，导致油价⼤幅拉升并带动美元和收益率同步⾛⾼，⾦价将⾯临回调压⼒，这是当
前最主要的下⾏⻛险。
中东局势是当前影响⾦价⾛向的最⼤变量，但其作⽤⽅向并⾮单⼀。
汇丰报告指出，中东冲突持续升级。国际能源署（ IEA ）亦警告称， " 在⽯油安全问题上没有任
何⾃满的空间 " ，并指出敌对⾏动的升级可能进⼀步压低全球⽯油库存。

该⾏认为，对⻩⾦⽽⾔，地缘政治紧张局势通常具有避险提振效应，但此次情况更为复杂。若
霍尔⽊兹海峡的⽯油运输受到冲击，油价⼤幅飙升将推⾼通胀预期，进⽽迫使美联储维持更⻓
时间的紧缩⽴场，美元和收益率的联动⾛强反⽽可能对⾦价形成压制。汇丰认为，这⼀传导链
条是当前⾦价⾯临的最⼤尾部⻛险。
值得注意的是，美联储⽬前正处于 “ 噤声 ” 期，市场缺乏来⾃货币政策层⾯的新增指引，这在⼀定
程度上加⼤了⾦价⾛势的不确定性。
技术⾯：中期偏空格局未破
摩根⼤通的技术分析为此轮上涨提供了更为审慎的解读框架。
Jason Hunter 在报告中指出，现货⾦价⽬前正在 4074 美元（ 2022 年 8 ⽉ 38.2% 斐波那契回撤位）
和 3886 美元（ 2025 年 10 ⽉低点）附近寻求⽀撑。近期出现的动量背离买⼊信号簇集，暗⽰市场
存在进⼀步盘整的可能，但这并不构成趋势反转的充分条件。
他列举了三项制约⾦价上⾏的结构性因素：

在这⼀背景下，摩根⼤通认为，⾦价在 4197 ⾄ 4264 美元的趋势线阻⼒群被突破之前，中期偏空
偏⻅依然有效。中期阻⼒位于 4500 美元附近。若⾦价重新加速下⾏，下⽅⽀撑依次位于 3605 美
元（ 2022 年 8 ⽉ 50% 回撤位）以及 3400 ⾄ 3500 美元的 2025 年四季度突破区域。
上⾏空间有限，但下⾏⻛险亦受制约
综合汇丰和摩根⼤通的观点，当前⾦价处于⼀个微妙的平衡区间 —— 既有⽀撑，也有顶压。
汇丰的 James Steel 倾向于认为⾦价将 " 缓慢⾛⾼ " ，理由是市场已基本消化货币政策收紧预期，
⽽其他⻛险因素整体偏向⽀撑⾦价。他同时指出，此前围绕半导体及电⼦产品需求的悲观预期
可能被过度定价， ETF 需求正在回暖，这些因素共同构成⾦价的底部⽀撑。

摩根⼤通则更为谨慎，认为夏季期间上⾏空间将受到明显压制，并将 4197 ⾄ 4264 美元视为近期
的关键阻⼒区间。只有有效突破这⼀区域，才能改变当前的中期偏空判断。
其⼀，图表上缺乏中期积累形态；其⼆，美元指数正在年线区间突破位上⽅运⾏，技术形态偏
多；其三， 2 年期美债收益率已突破多季度区间⽀撑，处于偏空位置。

【重要资讯】 
1、高盛认为，韩股在关键支撑位获机构净买入，散户割肉出局使筹码更干净；受
制于设备和零部件的结构性短缺，行业实际产能扩张幅度将远低于市场担忧的上
限，存储产能实际扩张将远不及预期。维持 KOSPI 目标价 12000 点，立场看多。 
2、巴克莱给予海力士"增持"评级，目标价 330 美元，核心逻辑三条：科技行业内
存持续短缺支撑涨价；SK 海力士在 HBM 领域的强势地位将带来显著定价溢价； 现金
储备预计达市值 40%以上，具备大规模回购能力。 
3、Citadel Securities 首席策略师：两周前预警的美股技术性重置已基本完成，
十项观测指标九项转绿。零售买盘创历史纪录、仓位压力系统性缓解、领涨范围扩
大，科技股估值甚至低于十年均值。二季度标普 500 EPS 预期同比增长 22.4%。 
4、阿斯麦将 2026 年全年净销售额预期上调至 430 亿至 450 亿欧元， 较此前360 亿
至 400 亿欧元的区间大幅提升。阿斯麦披露，英特尔已在美国俄勒冈州工厂正式将
High NA EUV 设备用于部分 Ultra 3（猎豹湖）处理器的量产制造。 
5、高盛警告，IBM 大跌事件将充分印证"软件熊市情景"， 预计软件与服务板块将出
现广泛抛售。在 AI 繁荣推高服务器、存储及内存供应紧张的背景下，企业客户将
大量预算临时转向抢购硬件基础设施，导致多笔大型合同未能如期签署。 
6、美国 6 月 CPI 同比涨 3.5%，虽然有所回落，但仍然大幅偏离美联储2%目标。分
析师指出，科技红利与全球化红利正在消退，人口老龄化持续推升通胀中枢，高通
胀或将成为新常态，美联储公信力、家庭财富与金融市场稳定性，或将同步承压。 
7、沃什重申通胀是种选择，承诺打破“黏性价格”；暗示90 年代生产力繁荣时的
低利率政策不应简单套用于当前 AI 投资热潮时；专注于就业和通胀双重使命，不
会涉足其他领域；联储不应随意干预市场，危机时利用资产负债表是例外。 
8、5 月外国投资者持有美债规模升至 9.37 万亿美元，创历史第二高。加拿大持仓
增加 387 亿美元领跑，英国持仓增加 111 亿美元、中国大陆持仓增加 82 亿美元；
但第一大持有国日本持仓减少 668 亿美元。

【全球经济逻辑】 
    高盛发布报告，建议客户将仓位从韩国AI 交易切换至“中国AI 价值链”，推
出覆盖电力、半导体、AI 基础设施等全链条的股票篮子。该行认为，中国 AI 市值
约 4 万亿美元，全球基金配置比例仅 1.2%，存在显著低配。中国AI 大模型凭借极
致性价比与逼近前沿的性能， 在美国市场Token 占比从一年前的不足2%飙升至46%，
超过 80%的美国 AI 初创企业正在使用中国开源模型。华为韬定律V2 版发布，国内
半导体产业链从韬定律 EDA 立体芯片设计、到逻辑折叠半导体设备、半导体材料，
都将迎来全新的发展机遇。韩国 800 万亿韩元存储集群计划引发周期见顶担忧，但
美银认为新产能最快 2033 年后才释放，短期供需无虞。市场将"宣布投资"等同于"
供给释放"，犯了严重的时间错配错误。韩国建厂在相当长时间内不会影响供需格
局。 三星集团发布投资计划， 宣布将投资11.68 万亿元人民币布局未来产业。 其中，
8.93 万亿元人民币将投向半导体产业集群建设。苹果被曝正游说美国政府， 寻求获
准采购长鑫存储内存芯片，真正驱动力是 2027 年全球内存供应缺口将持续扩大。
美国债务问题已越过不可逆转的临界点，债券供过于求推高长期利率，最终将美联
储逼入”加息伤经济、降息促通胀”的滞胀困局，债务危机已不可避免。 
达里欧警告称，AI 热潮已经呈现出典型的泡沫特征，泡沫最终会走向破裂。 
中国 AI 模型正以"白菜价"杀入美国企业市场——比 Anthropic、OpenAI 便宜
60%至 90%，性能差距却已缩至"六到九个月"。美企使用中国模型的占比从 4.5%骤
升至峰值 46%。 
高盛副董事长 Kaplan 发出严厉警告：美联储最早秋季重启加息，且极可能连
续出手 2 至3 次。 
由于美国连续的错误政策，全球经济已在 2025 年底越过顶部区域，持续向下
运行。

中共中央政治局委员、外交部⻓王毅 22 ⽇在⻢尼拉会⻅美国国务卿鲁⽐奥。
王毅表⽰，今年是中美关系的 “ ⼤年 ” 。两国元⾸在北京成功举⾏历史性会晤，确定了中美建设性
战略稳定关系的定位，提出了双⽅共同努⼒的⽅向。这是中美两个⼤国在探索和平共处道路上
取得的重要进展，符合两国⼈⺠根本利益，符合国际社会共同期待。现在我们的责任是，沿着
两国元⾸设定的轨道，排除⼲扰、克服障碍，确保将领导⼈的共识转化为全政府、各领域的共
识和⾏动，以中美的战略稳定促进世界的和平安宁，以中美的建设性互动为国际合作提供重要

动⼒。王毅还就近期美⽅的⼀系列消极⾔⾏阐明中⽅严正⽴场，要求美⽅尊重中国的核⼼利
益，恪守⼀个中国原则，切实管控⽭盾分歧，解决中⽅正当关切，让中美关系的机遇之年照进
现实。
双⽅还就国际地区热点问题交换了意⻅。
双⽅认为，此次会晤务实、积极、富有建设性，同意共同落实两国元⾸达成的重要共识，发挥
政治外交渠道作⽤，筹备好下阶段⾼层交往，推动中美建设性战略稳定关系取得实质进展。

株式市場～信用買いとレバレッジ型ETFが急増
 米株式市場はAIブームを背景に活況を呈する。そうした中、一段の株高を前提に収益を拡大させるため、信用買いにと
もなう債務残高や、対象とする株式の日々の値動きに2～3倍で連動するハイリスクなレバレッジ型上場投資信託の資
産残高が急増している。
 これらは、現下の株高がバブルであることを必ずしも示唆するものではない。しかし、かりに株安局面が到来すると、株
価下落にともなう損失が拡大し、逆資産効果により実体経済に与える影響も増幅させることに注意が必要であろう。

FOMC委員の経済金融見通し（18人の委員による予測の中央値）
 FRBは6月16、17日に開催した連邦公開市場委員会（FOMC）で、政策金利であるFF金利の誘導目標を3.5～3.75%
に据え置いた。 ウォーシュ新議長は会見で、物価安定を重視する姿勢を強調した。
 FOMC委員の経済金融見通しは、景気の底堅さを踏まえ、今年の成長率予測と、26～28年の物価見通しを上方修正
した。そのうえで金融政策については今年1回の利上げが想定されている。
 米金利先物からFOMC会合ごとの政策金利を予想するCMEの「フェドウオッチ」によると、米国、イスラエルのイラン攻
撃前は年内2回程度の利下げが織り込まれていたが、油価上昇によりインフレ懸念が強まったこともあり、直近では年
内1回程度の利上げが織り込まれている。実際の金利変更の有無やその時期が今後の焦点となる。

## Document 754: 重赏之下有勇夫！霍尔木兹海峡风险飙升，航运公司以高额奖金吸引船员冒险通行

Source PDF: all report pdf/重赏之下有勇夫！霍尔木兹海峡风险飙升，航运公司以高额奖金吸引船员冒险通行.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-07-22
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

霍尔⽊兹海峡⻛险持续升级，全球最⼤油轮船东之⼀ Sinokor 祭出相当于六个⽉薪资的⾼额
奖⾦招募船员穿越险区，但仍难抵安全忧虑。商船遇袭频发、船员离职增加、航运公司绕
⾏避险，全球能源运输成本与供应链压⼒进⼀步加剧。
摘要：
霍尔⽊兹海峡已成为全球最危险的航运⽔道之⼀，⽽航运公司正试图⽤⾦钱来弥补这⼀⻛险缺
⼝。
据彭博报道，包括全球最⼤超级油轮船东 Sinokor 集团在内的国际航运企业，正向愿意穿越霍尔
⽊兹海峡的船员提供⾼额奖⾦。⾃美伊冲突爆发以来，已有⾄少 59 艘商船在波斯湾及周边⽔域
遭到袭击， 17 名船员罹难。航运保险费率与船员奖⾦双双飙升，但仍有⼤量船员宁可放弃额外
报酬，也不愿冒险通⾏。
Kpler 最新航运数据显⽰， 7 ⽉ 17 ⽇⾄ 19 ⽇期间，经霍尔⽊兹海峡的经核实过境船只仅录得 30
艘，海峡通⾏量持续受到严重压制。与此同时，据路透社报道，航运公司正主动回避美国主导
的沿阿曼海岸通⾏⾛廊，担忧遭受伊朗打击，局势进⼀步加剧市场对全球能源运输安全的担
忧。

Sinokor 开出六个⽉薪资奖⾦
据彭博获得的⼀份⽂件显⽰， Sinokor 集团向船员提供相当于六个⽉额外薪资的奖⾦，条件是完
成⼀次从沙特阿拉伯或伊拉克装载原油、并在阿曼湾卸货的往返航程，该公司估计整个⾏程约
需⼀个⽉。
按照这⼀⽅案，船⻓可获得约 1.5 万美元的奖⾦，⽽普通船员的⽉薪仅约 1500 美元。值得注意的
是，Sinokor 对外收取的租船费⽤⾼达每⽇ 50 万美元，船员所获奖⾦与船东收益之间的悬殊差距
引发外界关注。
全球海员培训机构 GlobalMET 主席、同时与国际海事组织（ IMO ）存在合作关系的 Captain
Pradeep Chawla 表⽰， " 部分公司正在向船员提供巨额奖⾦ " ，并指出 " 我们听说有⼤量船员选择
下船离职，但公司仍能找到愿意前往的⼈ " 。
袭击频发，船员诉诸法律
海峡内的安全形势持续恶化，已引发法律层⾯的连锁反应。
今年 7 ⽉初，三名泰国船员对其前雇主 Precious Shipping 及两家关联公司、以及涉事船⻓提起诉

讼，指控对⽅将其置于危险境地，并在九个⽉合同期满前将其解雇。此前，今年 3 ⽉⼀枚弹射物
击中其所在货船，造成三名船员死亡。
联合国航运机构的数据显⽰，⾃美伊冲突爆发以来，波斯湾及周边⽔域已有⾄少 59 艘商船遭到
袭击， 17 名船员在事件中丧⽣。商船频繁遭袭直接导致霍尔⽊兹海峡通⾏量骤降，运输成本随
之⼤幅攀升。
美国主导通⾏⽅案效果存疑

据路透社报道，航运公司正在刻意绕开美国主导的、沿阿曼海岸线设⽴的霍尔⽊兹通⾏⾛廊，
原因是在伊美谅解备忘录（ MoU ）框架下，绕过伊朗指定航道的船只已多次遭到袭击。
⼀位航运业内⼈⼠表⽰，美国⽅⾯似乎对当前局势已失去掌控。⻛险咨询机构 Verisk Maplecroft
分析师 Torbjorn Solvedt 警告称，伊朗持续具备打击阿曼航线船只的能⼒，这使得特朗普政府维
持海峡通⾏的计划难以奏效。
当前局势表明，在地缘政治⻛险尚未实质性缓解的背景下，单纯依靠经济激励⼿段维系霍尔⽊
兹海峡航运秩序的空间极为有限，全球能源供应链所⾯临的压⼒短期内难以消散。
